```text
1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}). The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (fraction of questions with $>1$ distinct answer) is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.
45	\begin{equation}
46	\label{eq:c0-def}
47	C_0 = \frac{|\{q \in \mathcal{Q} : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}|}{|\mathcal{Q}|}.
48	\end{equation}
49	
50	\begin{figure}[t]
51	\centering
52	\small
53	\begin{tikzpicture}[
54	    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,
55	    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,
56	                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\scriptsize},
57	    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},
58	    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},
59	    lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},
60	    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,
61	                    inner sep=3pt},
62	]
63	\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};
64	\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\traces};
65	\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\constraints};
66	\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\ +\\weight};
67	\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\(Z3)};
68	
69	\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};
70	\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\vote};
71	
72	\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);
73	\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);
74	\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);
75	\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);
76	\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);
77	\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);
78	
79	\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};
80	
81	\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\scriptsize\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};
82	
83	\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};
84	\end{tikzpicture}
85	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
86	\label{fig:pipeline}
87	\end{figure}
88

The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (fraction of questions with $>1$ distinct answer) is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:c0-def}\nC_0 = \\frac{|\\{q \\in \\mathcal{Q} : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}|}{|\\mathcal{Q}|}.\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 88
    }

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into FOL for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. More recent systems extend this: DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos} selects solver types, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle} applies logic-complete decomposition, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover} trains FOL verifiers, and LogicAgent \citep{luo2025logicagent} employs multi-perspective grounding. Each introduces capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity, consistent with our findings. Our oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models intensify this (QwQ-32B: $C_0 = 2.6\%$). Trained verifiers offer an alternative: outcome reward models \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}, process reward models \citep{lightman2024prm}, and Math-Shepherd \citep{wang2024mathshepherd}. Multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our symbolic confirmation bias finding (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}): the same model that produces an error cannot reliably generate constraints that correct it.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this to reasoning-trace aggregation across 15 conditions (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold FOL annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \geq 200$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock time (81.4 vs.\ 17.7\,min for 204 questions). Constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls, 17.8\,s/question) accounts for 76\% of overhead; Z3 solving is negligible (3\,ms, ${<}0.1\%$). Among remediation methods, ICG costs $3.2{\times}$, Debate $10.5{\times}$, and Contrastive $10.9{\times}$.
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20	
21	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. SICA effectiveness depends sharply on the accuracy regime.
22	
23	\begin{table*}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold FOL examples. $\dagger$\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \textbf{Bold $\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\,$p < 0.05$;\enspace{**}\,$p < 0.01$;\enspace{***}\,$p < 0.001$. Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}.}
27	\label{tab:main_results}
28	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
29	\toprule
30	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
31	\midrule
32	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ 40\%)}} \\
33	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\
34	\midrule
35	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\sim$50--55\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\
36	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
37	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\

44	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\
45	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
46	Qwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 75.17 & 72.67 & $\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\

56	\midrule

58	Mistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \textbf{85.78} & $\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\times}10^{-13}${***} \\
59	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \textbf{89.71} & $\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\times}10^{-7}${***} \\
60	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \textbf{86.67} & $\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\times}10^{-15}${***} \\

64	
65	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--55\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect (entropy decomposition in Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}). Mistral-7B on LogiQA, despite the same SC range, yields only $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp ($p = 1.0$), attributable to domain formalizability (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}).
66	
67	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%): confirmation bias.} All nine Type~A conditions (SC $\geq 60\%$, combined $N > 2{,}800$) produce no significant positive effects. The largest positive trend is LLaMA-3.1-8B on StrategyQA ($\Delta = +2.00$\,pp, $p = 0.424$, n.s.), while Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). Even in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\Delta = +0.50$\,pp, n.s.) cannot break confirmation bias.
68	
69	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%): error amplification.} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning.
70	
71	\paragraph{Degenerate cases.} When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
72	
73	Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} visualizes the regime dependence: the intermediate-SC region is narrow and observed only for Mistral-7B on FOLIO.
74	
75	\begin{figure}[t]
76	\centering
77	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}
78	\caption{SICA $\Delta$ (pp) vs.\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: significant degradation ($p = 0.014$).}
79	\label{fig:regime-scatter}
80	\end{figure}
81	
82	
83	\subsection{Oracle Control}
84	\label{sec:oracle}
85	
86	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: FOL from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Same-model Oracle-SICA on Mistral-7B achieves 85.78\% on FOLIO ($\Delta = +29.90$\,pp, $p < 10^{-13}$); Qwen2.5-14B achieves 89.71\% ($\Delta = +14.22$\,pp, $p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$) and 86.67\% on ProofWriter ($\Delta = +11.50$\,pp, $p = 9.21 \times 10^{-15}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The larger ceiling for weaker models ($+29.90$ vs.\ $+14.22$\,pp) is consistent with the bias ratio gradient (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
87

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}.     "oldString": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}",
    "newString": "\\section{Background, Setup \\& Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:method}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (fraction of questions with $>1$ distinct answer) is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:c0-def}\nC_0 = \\frac{|\\{q \\in \\mathcal{Q} : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}|}{|\\mathcal{Q}|}.\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",
